On the sharp critical mass threshold for the 3D Patlak-Keller-Segel-Navier-Stokes system via Couette flow
Shikun Cui, Lili Wang, Wendong Wang, Juncheng Wei

Abstract
. As is well-known, the solution of the Patlak-Keller-Segel system in 3D may blow up in finite time regardless of any initial cell mass. In this paper, we are interested in the suppression of blow-up and the critical mass threshold for the 3D Patlak-Keller-Segel-Navier-Stokes system via the Couette flow ( A ​ y , 0 , 0 ) (Ay,0,0) . It is proved that if the Couette flow is sufficiently strong ( A A is large enough), then the solutions for the system are global in time in the periodic domain ( x , y , z ) ∈ 𝕋 3 (x,y,z)\in\mathbb{T}^{3} as long as the initial cell mass is less than 16 ​ π 2 16\pi^{2} . This result seems to be sharp, since the zero-mode function (the mean value in x − x- direction) of the three dimensional density is a complication of the two-dimensional Keller-Segel equations, whose critical mass in 2D is 8 ​ π 8\pi . One new observation is the dissipative decay of ( u ~ 2 , 0 , u ~ 3 , 0 ) (\widetilde{u}_{2,0},\widetilde{u}_{3,0}) (see Lemma 4.3 for more details), then we combine the quasi-linear method proposed by Wei-Zhang (Comm. Pure Appl. Math., 2021) with the zero-mode estimate of the density by the logarithmic Hardy-Littlewood-Sobolev inequality as Bedrossian-He (SIAM J. Math. Anal., 2017) or He (Nonlinearity, 2025) to obtain the bounded-ness of the density and the velocity.



Keywords:
suppression of blow-up; Patlak-Keller-Segel-Navier-Stokes; stability;
Couette flow

1.
Introduction

Consider the following parabolic-elliptic Patlak-Keller-Segel (PKS) system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\partial_{t}n=\triangle n-\nabla\cdot(n\nabla c),\\
\triangle c+n=0,\end{array}\right.

(1.1)

which is designed to depict the diffusion and chemotactic motion of chemical substances within a population of cells or microorganisms.
Patlak made significant contributions in
[
25
]
, and later, Keller and Segel further advanced it in
[
21
]
. Extensive applications have been found across diverse scientific fields, including biology, ecology, and medicine
[
17
]
.
In the realm of biology, this system provides crucial insights into the complex behavior of cells, such as their migration, aggregation, and diffusion
[
16
]
.

A well-known characteristic of the PKS system is its critical dependence on spatial dimension.
For the one-dimensional PKS system, all its solutions are globally well-posed.
When the spacial dimension is higher than one, the solutions of the PKS system (
1.1
) may blow up in finite time. In the two-dimensional space, the PKS system for both parabolic-elliptic and parabolic-parabolic forms have a critical mass of
8\pi
. In the 2D space, the parabolic-elliptic PKS system is globally well-posed if and only if the total mass
M\leq 8\pi
by Wei
[
33
]
, see also Blanchet-Dolbeault-Perthame
[
3
]
for
M<8\pi
. When
M=8\pi
, the solution may blow up at infinity, see Blanchet-Carrillo-Masmoudi
[
2
]
and Davila-del Pino-Dolbeault-Musso-Wei
[
9
]
for the blow-up rate at infinity.
The parabolic-parabolic PKS model (
\triangle c
is replaced by
\triangle c-\partial_{t}c
in
\eqref{eq:ks}_{2}
) also has a critical mass of
8\pi
in 2D: if the cell mass
M:=||n_{\rm in}||_{L^{1}}
is less than
8\pi
, the solutions of the system are global in time proved by Calvez-Corrias
[
4
]
;
if the cell mass is greater than
8\pi
, the solutions will blow up in finite time proved by Schweyer
[
26
]
.

When the spatial dimension is higher than two, the PKS system (
1.1
) becomes supercritical.
In this condition, regardless of parabolic-elliptic form or parabolic-parabolic form, the finite-time blow-up may occur for arbitrarily
small values of the initial mass.
In this time, the solution with any initial mass may blow up in finite time. This behavior has been established in various settings: for the parabolic-elliptic case by Nagai
[
24
]
and Souplet-Winkler
[
29
]
, and for the parabolic-parabolic case by Winkler
[
35
]
. Additional results and further developments on this topic can be found in
[
2
,
9
,
31
]
and the related therein.

Generally, the processes involving chemical attraction take place in fluids. As said in
[
22
]
:

A natural question is whether the presence of fluid flow
can affect singularity formation by mixing the bacteria thus making concentration
harder to achieve.

In this paper, we investigate the suppression of blow-up and the critical mass threshold of the following three-dimensional parabolic-elliptic Patlak-Keller-Segel (PKS) system coupled with Navier-Stokes (NS) equations in
(x,y,z)\in\mathbb{T}^{3}
with
\mathbb{T}=[0,2\pi]
:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\partial_{t}n+v\cdot\nabla n=\triangle n-\nabla\cdot(n\nabla c),\\
\triangle c+n-\bar{n}=0,\\
\partial_{t}v+v\cdot\nabla v+\nabla P=\triangle v+n\nabla\phi,\\
\nabla\cdot v=0,\end{array}\right.

(1.2)

along with initial conditions

(n,v)\big|_{t=0}=(n_{\rm in},v_{\rm in}),

where
n
represents the cell density,
c
denotes the chemoattractant density, and
v
denotes the velocity of fluid. In addition,
\bar{n}=\frac{1}{|\mathbb{T}|^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}ndxdydz
denotes the average of
n
,
P
is the pressure and
\phi
is the given potential function.

Let us by briefly review some results obtained in the 2D case, which implies the
8\pi
critical mass threshold vanishes by the mixing effect of the fluid.
For the parabolic-elliptic PKS system of
(\ref{ini})_{1}-(\ref{ini})_{2}
, Kiselev-Xu
[
22
]
showed no critical mass threshold by suppressing the blow-up by stationary relaxation
enhancing flows and time-dependent Yao-Zlatos near-optimal mixing flows in
\mathbb{T}^{d}
with
d=2,3
. Bedrossian-He
[
1
]
studied the suppression of blow-up by non-degenerate shear flows in

\mathbb{T}^{2}
for the 2D parabolic-elliptic case. He
[
12
]
investigated the suppression of blow-up for the parabolic-parabolic PKS model near the large strictly monotone shear flow in
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}
. For the coupled PKS-NS system, Zeng-Zhang-Zi
[
36
]
firstly considered the 2D PKS-NS system near the Couette flow in
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}
, and
proved that if the Couette flow is sufficiently strong, the solution stays globally regular. He
[
13
]
considered the blow-up suppression for the parabolic-elliptic PKS-NS system in
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}
with the coupling of buoyancy effects for a class of initial data with small vorticity.
Wang-Wang-Zhang
[
32
]
studied the blow-up suppression of 2D supercritical parabolic-elliptic PKS-NS system near the Couette flow in
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}.

Li-Xiang-Xu
[
23
]
suppressed the blow-up for the PKS-NS system via the Poiseuille flow in
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R},

and proved that the solution is global as long as the Poiseuille flow is enough strong.
Furthermore, Cui-Wang
[
6
]
considered the blow-up suppression for the PKS-NS system in
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{I}
with Navier-slip boundary condition.
In addition, Hu
[
18
]
proved that sufficiently large buoyancy can also suppress the blow-up of the PKS system, see also the recent results by Hu-Kiselev-Yao
[
20
]
and Hu-Kiselev
[
19
]
. The blow-up also can be suppressed by adding some logistic terms, see
[
30
]
and the references therein.

In 3D, some results have been made in the study of the corresponding problem.
Bedrossian-He
[
1
]
studied the suppression of blow-up for the parabolic-elliptic PKS system by shear flows in
\mathbb{T}^{3}
and
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
Feng-Shi-Wang
[
11
]
suppressed the blow-up for the advective Kuramoto-Sivashinsky and
the Keller-Segel equations via the planar helical flows. Shi-Wang
[
28
]
considered the suppression effect of the Couette-Poiseuille flow
(z,z^{2},0)
in
\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
, and Deng-Shi-Wang
[
10
]
proved the Couette flow with a sufficiently large amplitude prevents the blow-up of solutions in the whole space for exponential decay data. For the parabolic-parabolic PKS system, He
[
14
]
introduced a family of time-dependent alternating shear flows in the domain
\mathbb{T}^{3}
, and proved the solution remains globally regular as long as the flow is sufficiently strong. For a time-dependent shear flow, He
[
15
]
demonstrated that when the total
mass of the cell density is below a specific threshold (
8\pi|\mathbb{T}|
), the solution remains globally regular in
\mathbb{T}^{3}
as long as the
flow is sufficiently strong.

For the 3D PKS-NS system, fewer results are obtained. The first three authors
[
7
]
considered the PKS system coupled with
the linearized NS equations near the Couette flow in
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{T},
and showed that
when
A
is big enough the solutions are global in time as long as
M<\frac{8\pi}{9}
and
A(\|u_{2,\rm in}\|_{L^{2}}+\|u_{3,\rm in}\|_{L^{2}})\leq C.

Then, the first three authors
[
8
]
studied the blow-up suppression and the nonlinear stability of the PKS-NS system for the Couette flow
(Ay,0,0)
in
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T},
and proved that
the solutions are global in time as long as the initial cell mass
M<\frac{24}{5}\pi^{2}
and initial velocity
A^{\frac{1}{3}+}\|u_{\rm in}\|_{H^{2}}\leq C
.

Our main goal is to investigate the suppression of blow-up and obtain the optimal critical mass for the PKS-NS system (
1.2
) near the 3D Couette flow
(Ay,0,0)
. Introduce a perturbation
u=(u_{1},u_{2},u_{3})
around the Couette flow
(Ay,0,0)
, which
u(t,x,y,z)=v(t,x,y,z)-(Ay,0,0)
satisfying
u\big|_{t=0}=u_{\rm in}=(u_{1,\rm in},u_{2,\rm in},u_{3,\rm in})
. Assume
\phi=x,
then we rewrite the system (
1.2
) into

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\partial_{t}n+Ay\partial_{x}n+u\cdot\nabla n-\triangle n=-\nabla\cdot(n\nabla c),\\
\triangle c+n-\bar{n}=0,\\
\partial_{t}u+Ay\partial_{x}u+\left(\begin{array}[]{c}Au_{2}\\
0\\
0\\
\end{array}\right)-\triangle u+u\cdot\nabla u+\nabla P^{N_{1}}+\nabla P^{N_{2}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}n\\
0\\
0\\
\end{array}\right),\\
\nabla\cdot u=0,\end{array}\right.

(1.3)

where the pressure
P^{N_{1}}
and
P^{N_{2}}
are determined by

\displaystyle\triangle P^{N_{1}}=-2A\partial_{x}u_{2}+\partial_{x}n,

(1.4)

\displaystyle\triangle P^{N_{2}}=-{\rm div}~(u\cdot\nabla u).

To estimate non-zero norms more conveniently, we introduce the vorticity
\omega_{2}=\partial_{z}u_{1}-\partial_{x}u_{3}
and
\triangle u_{2},
satisfying

\partial_{t}\omega_{2}+Ay\partial_{x}\omega_{2}+A\partial_{z}u_{2}-\triangle\omega_{2}=\partial_{z}n-\partial_{z}(u\cdot\nabla u_{1})+\partial_{x}(u\cdot\nabla u_{3})

and

\partial_{t}\triangle u_{2}+Ay\partial_{x}\triangle u_{2}-\triangle^{2}u_{2}=-\partial_{y}\partial_{x}n-(\partial_{x}^{2}+\partial_{z}^{2})(u\cdot\nabla u_{2})+\partial_{y}[\partial_{x}(u\cdot\nabla u_{1})+\partial_{z}(u\cdot\nabla u_{3})].

After the time rescaling
t\mapsto\frac{t}{A}
, we get

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\partial_{t}n+y\partial_{x}n+\frac{1}{A}u\cdot\nabla n-\frac{1}{A}\triangle n=-\frac{1}{A}\nabla\cdot(n\nabla c),\\
\triangle c+n-\bar{n}=0,\\
\partial_{t}\omega_{2}+y\partial_{x}\omega_{2}-\frac{1}{A}\triangle\omega_{2}+\partial_{z}u_{2}=-\frac{1}{A}\partial_{z}(u\cdot\nabla u_{1})+\frac{1}{A}\partial_{x}(u\cdot\nabla u_{3})+\frac{1}{A}\partial_{z}n,\\
\partial_{t}\triangle u_{2}+y\partial_{x}\triangle u_{2}-\frac{1}{A}\triangle(\triangle u_{2})=-\frac{1}{A}\partial_{y}\partial_{x}n-\frac{1}{A}(\partial_{x}^{2}+\partial_{z}^{2})(u\cdot\nabla u_{2})\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{A}\partial_{y}\left[\partial_{x}(u\cdot\nabla u_{1})+\partial_{z}(u\cdot\nabla u_{3})\right],\\
{\nabla\cdot u=0.}\end{array}\right.

(1.5)

Before stating the result, we need to define the following modes

P_{0}f=f_{0}=\frac{1}{|\mathbb{T}|}\int_{\mathbb{T}}f(t,x,y,z)dx,~~~{\rm and}~~~P_{\neq}f=f_{\neq}=f-f_{0}.

Throughout this paper,
f_{0}
and
f_{\neq}
respectively represent the zero and non-zero modes of
f.

Our main result is stated as follows.

Theorem 1.1
.

Assume that
0<n_{\rm in}(x,y,z)\in H^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{3})
and
u_{\rm in}(x,y,z)\in H^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{3}).

There exist a sufficiently small positive constant
\epsilon
depending on

\|n_{\rm in}\|_{H^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{3})}
and
\|(u_{\rm in})_{\neq}\|_{H^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{3})},

and a positive constant
A_{1}
depending on
\|n_{\rm in}\|_{H^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{3})}
and
\|u_{\rm in}\|_{H^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{3})}
,
such that if
A\geq A_{1},

\|(u_{2,\rm in})_{0}\|_{H^{2}}+\|(u_{3,\rm in})_{0}\|_{H^{1}}\leq\epsilon

(1.6)

and

M=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}n_{\rm in}dxdydz<16\pi^{2}.

(1.7)

Then the solution of (
1.5
) is global in time.

Remark 1.1
.

For the three-dimensional space, the index
16\pi^{2}
in (
1.7
) seems to be the sharp threshold for initial cell mass.
Recall that
n_{0}
satisfies (see also (
6.16
))

\displaystyle\partial_{t}n_{0}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{A}\triangle n_{0}-\frac{1}{A}\nabla\cdot(n_{0}\nabla c_{0})-\frac{1}{A}\nabla\cdot(n_{\neq}\nabla c_{\neq})_{0}-\frac{1}{A}(u_{0}\cdot\nabla n_{0})-\frac{1}{A}(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla n_{\neq})_{0}.

(1.8)

When the velocity
u
vanishes and
n(t,x,y,z)=n(t,y,z)
, which does not depend on the variable
x
. Then (
1.8
) is reduced to

\displaystyle\partial_{t}n_{0}=\frac{1}{A}\triangle n_{0}-\frac{1}{A}\nabla\cdot(n_{0}\nabla c_{0})+``{\rm good~terms}",

(1.9)

which is similar to the 2D Keller-Segel equations and the critical mass is
\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}n_{0}dydz=8\pi=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}ndxdydz
. It implies that the critical mass threshold for the initial cell mass to the system (
1.8
) is just
16\pi^{2}
. A new observation is the logarithmic Hardy-Littlewood-Sobolev inequality and the dissipative decay of the
(\widetilde{u}_{2,0},\widetilde{u}_{3,0})
(see Lemma
4.3
in Section 4.2 for more details) to estimate the norm of
\|n_{0}\|_{L^{2}}
.

Remark 1.2
.

The small constant
\epsilon
in (
1.6
) depends on
\|n_{\rm in}\|_{H^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{3})}
and
\|(u_{\rm in})_{\neq}\|_{H^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{3})},
which can be found in (
6.8
), that is
\epsilon m^{\frac{1}{3}}E_{3}^{\frac{5}{3}}\leq C
. Moreover, the estimate of
E_{3}
is decided by (
6.14
),
(
7.4
) and
Corollary
8.1
.

Remark 1.3
.

In a very recent work
[
8
]
, the first three authors investigated the suppression of blow-up and the nonlinear stability of the PKS-NS system via the 3D Couette flow
in a different domain of
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}
and
\phi=y
. When
A
is big enough, as long as

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}A^{\frac{1}{3}+}(\|u_{\rm in}\|_{H^{2}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})}+\|(n_{\rm in})_{(0,\neq)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})})\leq C,\\
M=\int_{\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}}n_{\rm in}dxdydz<\frac{24}{5}\pi^{2},\end{array}\right.

the solutions for the PKS-NS system are global in time, where a new energy estimate for
\frac{\partial_{z}}{\sqrt{1-\triangle}}n_{(0,\neq)}
was introduced to control the density. It’s still unknown whether the threshold for initial cell mass can be
16\pi^{2}
, since it is difficult to apply the logarithmic Hardy-Littlewood-Sobolev inequality without the dissipative decay of the
(u_{2,0},u_{3,0})
at this time.

Remark 1.4
.

According to the standard arguments, the result of local well-posedness of the system (
1.5
) exists, which can be refered to
[
8
,
19
,
35
]
, and we omitted it.

Here are some notations used in this paper.

Notations
:

•

For given
f(t,x,y,z)
, the Fourier transform can be defined by

f(t,x,y,z)=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{f}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}(t)e^{i\left(k_{1}x+k_{2}y+k_{3}z\right)},

where

\widehat{f}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}(t)=\frac{1}{|\mathbb{T}|^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}f(t,x,y,z)e^{-i\left(k_{1}x+k_{2}y+k_{3}z\right)}dxdydz
.

•

Especially, we use
u_{j,0}
, and
u_{j,\neq}
to represent the zero mode and non-zero mode of the velocity
u_{j}(j=1,2,3)
, respectively. Similarly, we use
\omega_{2,0}
and
\omega_{2,\neq}
to represent the zero mode and non-zero mode of the vorticity
\omega_{2}
, respectively.

•

The norm of the
L^{p}
space and the time-space norm
\|f\|_{L^{q}L^{p}}
are defined as
\|f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{3})}=\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|f|^{p}dxdydz\right)^{\frac{1}{p}},

and

\|f\|_{L^{q}L^{p}}=\left\|\|f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{3})}\ \right\|_{L^{q}(0,t)}.

Moreover,
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
denotes the standard
L^{2}
scalar product. For simplicity, we write
\|f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{3})}
as
\|f\|_{L^{p}}.

•

For
a>0,
we define the norms

\displaystyle\|f\|_{X_{a}}^{2}=\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}f\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\nabla\triangle^{-1}\partial_{x}f\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}+\frac{\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}f\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}}{A^{\frac{1}{3}}}+\frac{\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\nabla f\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}}{A},

\displaystyle\|f\|_{Y_{0}}^{2}=\|f\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+\frac{1}{A}\|\nabla f\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}.

•

We sometimes denote the partial derivatives
\partial_{x},

\partial_{y}
and
\partial_{z}
by

\partial_{1},

\partial_{2}
and
\partial_{3},
respectively.

•

In this paper, unless otherwise specified, we use the Einstein summation convention.

•

The total mass
\|n(t)\|_{L^{1}}
is denoted by
M,
and let
m:=\|n_{0}\|_{L^{1}}.
Clearly,

\displaystyle M:=\|n(t)\|_{L^{1}}=\|n_{\rm in}\|_{L^{1}},

\displaystyle m:=\|(n_{\rm in})_{0}\|_{L^{1}}=\frac{\|n\|_{L^{1}}}{|\mathbb{T}|}=\frac{M}{|\mathbb{T}|}.

•

Throughout this paper, we denote
C
by a positive constant independent of
A
,
t
and the initial data, and it may be different from line to line.

2.
Key ideas and proof of Theorem
1.1

2.1.
Fourier analysis

For the given function
f
, by Fourier series, we get

f(t,x,y,z)=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{f}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}(t){\rm e}^{i\left(k_{1}x+k_{2}y+k_{3}z\right)},

where
\widehat{f}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}(t)=\frac{1}{|\mathbb{T}|^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}f(t,x,y,z){\rm e}^{-i\left(k_{1}x+k_{2}y+k_{3}z\right)}dxdydz.

According to the frequency
k_{1},

f
can be decomposed into

f=f_{\neq}+f_{0},

where

\displaystyle f_{\neq}(t,y,z)=\sum_{k_{2},k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z},k_{1}\neq 0}\widehat{f}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}(t){\rm e}^{i\left(k_{1}x+k_{2}y+k_{3}z\right)},~~f_{0}(t,y,z)=\sum_{k_{2},k_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{f}_{0,k_{2},k_{3}}(t){\rm e}^{i\left(k_{2}y+k_{3}z\right)}.

In fact,
f_{0}
is the zero mode, and
f_{\neq}
is the non-zero mode.

Furthermore, according to the frequency
k_{3},
the zero mode
f_{0}
can be decomposed into two parts
f_{0}=\overline{f}_{0}+\widetilde{f}_{0}
satisfying

\displaystyle\overline{f}_{0}(t)=\widehat{f}_{0,0,0}(t)=\frac{1}{{|\mathbb{T}|}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}f(t,x,y,z)dxdydz,

(2.1)

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{0}(t,y,z)=\sum_{k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}\neq 0}\widehat{f}_{0,k_{2},k_{3}}(t){\rm e}^{i\left(k_{2}y+k_{3}z\right)},

where
\overline{f}_{0}(t)
is called the average of
f
in the periodic domain
\mathbb{T}^{3}.

Assume that
f
satisfying

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\partial_{t}f+y\partial_{x}f-\frac{1}{A}\triangle f=0,\\
f|_{t=0}=f_{\rm in}.\end{array}\right.

The function
f
is divided into three different modes:

f(t,x,y,z)=f_{\neq}(t,x,y,z)+\widetilde{f}_{0}(t,y,z)+\overline{f}_{0}(t).

Then we consider the dynamics for different modes.
The non-zero mode
f_{\neq}
experiences the enhanced dissipation
[
2
]
:

\|f_{\neq}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq C{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\|(f_{\rm in})_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}.

The non-average part
\widetilde{f}_{0}
experiences the heat dissipation
with

\|\widetilde{f}_{0}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq{\rm e}^{-\frac{t}{2A}}\|\widetilde{f_{\rm in}}\|_{L^{2}}.

The average part
\overline{f}_{0}(t)
is a constant satisfying

\overline{f}_{0}(t)=\overline{(f_{\rm in})_{0}}.

What we need to emphasize is that the decomposition of different modes and the application of the corresponding dynamics are the keys to research the blow-up suppression problem in the periodic domain
\mathbb{T}^{3}
.

2.2.
The decomposition of the first component of the velocity

The first component
u_{1,0}
is affected by the 3D lift-up effect and is the worst part (see Section 2.1 in
[
5
]
). Therefore, we must handle it more carefully and meticulously.

Recall that
u_{1,0}
satisfies

\partial_{t}u_{1,0}-\frac{1}{A}\triangle u_{1,0}+u_{2,0}=-\frac{u_{2,0}\partial_{y}u_{1,0}+u_{3,0}\partial_{z}u_{1,0}}{A}-\frac{(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{1,\neq})_{0}}{A}+\frac{n_{0}}{A}.

First of all, we decompose
u_{1,0}
into

u_{1,0}(t,y,z)=\mathbf{G}_{1}(t,y,z)+\mathbf{B}_{1}(t,y,z)+\mathbf{B}_{2}(t,y,z),
satisfying

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbf{G}_{1}-\frac{1}{A}\triangle\mathbf{G}_{1}=-\frac{u_{2,0}\partial_{y}\mathbf{G}_{1}+u_{3,0}\partial_{z}\mathbf{G}_{1}}{A}-\frac{(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{1,\neq})_{0}}{A},

(2.2)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}_{1}-\frac{1}{A}\triangle\mathbf{B}_{1}=-\frac{u_{2,0}\partial_{y}\mathbf{B}_{1}+u_{3,0}\partial_{z}\mathbf{B}_{1}}{A}+\frac{n_{0}}{A},

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbf{B}_{2}-\frac{1}{A}\triangle\mathbf{B}_{2}+u_{2,0}=-\frac{u_{2,0}\partial_{y}\mathbf{B}_{2}+u_{3,0}\partial_{z}\mathbf{B}_{2}}{A},

along with the initial conditions

\mathbf{G}_{1}|_{t=0}=(u_{1,\rm in})_{0},\quad\mathbf{B}_{1}|_{t=0}=0,\quad\mathbf{B}_{2}|_{t=0}=0.

In this way,
\mathbf{G}_{1}(t,y,z)
is a good term
satisfying the following energy estimate

\|\mathbf{G}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}+\frac{1}{A}\|\nabla\mathbf{G}_{1}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}\leq C(\|(u_{1,\rm in})_{0}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}).

In addition, according to the frequency
k_{3}
, we decompose
\mathbf{B}_{1}(t,y,z)
and
\mathbf{B}_{2}(t,y,z)
into two parts

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}_{1}(t,y,z)=\overline{\mathbf{B}}_{1}(t)+\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{1}(t,y,z),

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}_{2}(t,y,z)=\overline{\mathbf{B}}_{2}(t)+\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}(t,y,z),

satisfying

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{1}(t,y,z)-\frac{1}{A}\triangle\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{1}(t,y,z)=-\frac{1}{A}(u_{2,0}\partial_{y}\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{1}+u_{3,0}\partial_{z}\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{1})+\frac{1}{A}\widetilde{n}_{0}(t,y,z),\\
\partial_{t}\overline{\mathbf{B}}_{1}(t)=\frac{1}{A}\overline{n}_{0}=\frac{M}{A|\mathbb{T}|^{3}},\end{array}\right.

(2.3)

and

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}(t,y,z)-\frac{1}{A}\triangle\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}(t,y,z)+\widetilde{u}_{2,0}(t,y,z)=-\frac{1}{A}(u_{2,0}\partial_{y}\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}+u_{3,0}\partial_{z}\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}),\\
\partial_{t}\overline{\mathbf{B}}_{2}(t)+\overline{u}_{2,0}(t)=0.\end{array}\right.

(2.4)

Lastly, we rewrite above decompositions into

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{1}(t,y,z)=\mathbf{G}_{1}(t,y,z)+\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{1}(t,y,z),

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{2}(t,y,z)=\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}(t,y,z)+\overline{\mathbf{B}}_{2}(t)+\overline{\mathbf{B}}_{1}(t),

satisfying
\mathbf{U}_{1}(t,y,z)+\mathbf{U}_{2}(t,y,z)=u_{1,0}(t,y,z).

In this way, we decompose the velocity
u_{1,0}
into the good part
\mathbf{U}_{1}(t,y,z)
and the bad part
\mathbf{U}_{2}(t,y,z)
satisfying

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}_{1}-\frac{1}{A}\triangle\mathbf{U}_{1}=-\frac{1}{A}(u_{2,0}\partial_{y}\mathbf{U}_{1}+u_{3,0}\partial_{z}\mathbf{U}_{1})+\frac{1}{A}\widetilde{n}_{0}(t,y,z)-\frac{(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{1,\neq})_{0}}{A},\\
\mathbf{U}_{1}|_{t=0}=(u_{1,\rm in})_{0},\end{array}\right.

and

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}_{2}-\frac{1}{A}\triangle\mathbf{U}_{2}+{u}_{2,0}=-\frac{1}{A}(u_{2,0}\partial_{y}\mathbf{U}_{2}+u_{3,0}\partial_{z}\mathbf{U}_{2})+\frac{M}{A|\mathbb{T}|^{3}},\\
\mathbf{U}_{2}|_{t=0}=0.\end{array}\right.

For the good velocity
\mathbf{U}_{1},

the
H^{1}
norm is enough for us to finish all calculations,
and it has a good relation with the cell density
n_{0}

by

\|\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}\leq C(\|(u_{1,\rm in})_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|n_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+\epsilon).

For the bad velocity
\mathbf{U}_{2}
, when it satisfies

\frac{\|\triangle\mathbf{U}_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{2}}}{A}+\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}}<c

for some small
c,
the operator
\mathcal{L}_{V}
can be regarded as perturbation of
\mathcal{L}
, which allow us to finish the time-space
estimates for the zero modes.
Here,
\mathcal{L}_{V}
and
\mathcal{L}

can be found in (
5.10
).

2.3.
The construction of energy functional

Firstly, we introduce the energy functional

\displaystyle E_{1,1}(t)=\|u_{2,0}\|_{Y_{0}}+\|u_{3,0}\|_{Y_{0}}+\|\nabla u_{2,0}\|_{Y_{0}}+\|\nabla u_{3,0}\|_{Y_{0}}+\|\triangle u_{2,0}\|_{Y_{0}}

\displaystyle+\|\min\{(A^{-\frac{2}{3}}+A^{-1}t)^{\frac{1}{2}},1\}\triangle u_{3,0}\|_{Y_{0}},

\displaystyle E_{1,2}(t)=A^{-1}(\|\triangle\mathbf{U}_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{2}}+A^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\|\nabla\triangle\mathbf{U}_{2}\|_{L^{2}H^{2}}})+\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{2}},

\displaystyle E_{2,1}(t)=\|\partial_{x}^{2}n_{\neq}\|_{X_{b}},

\displaystyle E_{2,2}(t)=\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}+\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}+A^{-\frac{1}{3}}\left(\|\partial_{y}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}+\|\partial_{z}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}\right),

\displaystyle E_{3}(t)=\|n\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}},

\displaystyle E_{4}(t)=\|\partial_{x}^{2}u_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{b}}+\|\partial_{x}^{2}u_{3,\neq}\|_{X_{b}},

where
a
and
b
are given positive constants with
0<a<b<2a
. Moreover, we denote

E_{1}(t)=E_{1,1}(t)+E_{1,2}(t),\quad E_{2}(t)=E_{2,1}(t)+E_{2,2}(t).

To close the energy, when estimating the nonlinear interactions between the non-zero modes of the velocity of
\|e^{2aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\nabla(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{3,\neq})\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}
, it is required that the degree of
A
must be strictly less than
\frac{5}{3}
(see Lemma
3.2
). To this end, we introduce the first auxiliary energy

E_{5,1}(t)=A^{-\frac{2}{3}}\|\triangle u_{3,\neq}\|_{X_{b}}.

Besides, to estimate
E_{4}
and handle the nonlinear interaction between the bad component
\mathbf{U}_{2}
of
u_{1,0}
and the non-zero mode
u_{\neq},
we also need the second auxiliary energy

\displaystyle E_{5,2}(t)=\sum_{j=2}^{3}\left(\|\partial_{x}^{2}u_{j,\neq}\|_{X_{b}}+\|\partial_{x}(\partial_{z}-\kappa\partial_{y})u_{j,\neq}\|_{X_{b}}\right)+\|\partial_{x}\nabla W\|_{X_{b}},

where the definition of
\kappa
and the expression of
W
can be found in Section
8
.

Let us briefly describe the roles of the above-mentioned energy functionals.

•

E_{1}
is introduced to control the zero modes of the velocity
u_{0}.

E_{1,1}
is used to control the good velocities
u_{2,0}
and
u_{3,0}.

E_{1,2}
is used to control the bad part
\mathbf{U}_{2}
of the velocity
u_{1,0}.

•

E_{2}
is introduced to deal with the non-zero mode of the cell density
n_{\neq}
and the velocity
u_{\neq}.

•

E_{3}
is introduced to estimate
\|n_{0}(t)\|_{L^{2}}

and deal with the nonlinear interaction items in the density
n
.

•

E_{4}
is introduced to deal with the nonlinear interaction items with 3D lift-up effect in
u_{\neq}
.

2.4.
Main steps

Proof of
Theorem
1.1
.

We prove Theorem
1.1
in the following three steps.

Step 1:
Let’s designate
T
as the terminal point of the largest range
[0,T]
such that the following hypothesis hold

E_{1}(t)\leq 2E_{1},\quad E_{2}(t)\leq 2E_{2},\quad E_{3}(t)\leq 2E_{3},\quad E_{4}(t)\leq 2E_{4},\quad E_{5,1}(t)\leq 2E_{5}

(2.5)

for any
t\in[0,T],
where
E_{1},E_{2},E_{3},E_{4}
and
E_{5}
are constants independent of
t
and
A
, and will be decided during the calculation.

Step 2:
We need to prove the following propositions:

Proposition 2.1
.

Assume that the initial data
(n_{\rm in},u_{\rm in})
satisfy the assumptions of Theorem
1.1
and (
2.5
), there exists a positive constant
A_{2}
independent of
A
and
t,
such that if
A\geq A_{2},
there holds

E_{1}(t)\leq E_{1}

for all
t\in[0,T].

Proposition 2.2
.

Assume that the initial data
(n_{\rm in},u_{\rm in})
satisfy the assumptions of Theorem
1.1
and (
2.5
), there exists a positive constant
A_{5}
independent of
A
and
t,
such that if
A\geq A_{5},
there holds

E_{3}(t)\leq E_{3}

for all
t\in[0,T].

Proposition 2.3
.

Assume that the initial data
(n_{\rm in},u_{\rm in})
satisfy the assumptions of Theorem
1.1
and (
2.5
), there exists a positive constant
A_{7}
independent of
A
and
t,
such that if
A\geq A_{7},
there holds

\displaystyle E_{4}(t)+E_{5,1}(t)

\displaystyle\leq E_{4}+E_{5},

\displaystyle E_{2}(t)

\displaystyle\leq E_{2},

for all
t\in[0,T].

Step 3:
Combining the above propositions with the well-posedness of system (
1.5
) given by Remark
1.4
, and taking
A_{1}=\max\{A_{2},A_{5},A_{7}\},
we complete the proof.

∎

3.
A priori estimates

Firstly, we give some relationships between velocity
u_{\neq}
and the new vorticity
\omega_{2,\neq}
, which will be frequently used in the later calculations.
Since
{\rm div}~u_{\neq}=0
, the result follows immediately from Fourier series (see Lemma 3.13 in
[
8
]
), we omit the proof.

Lemma 3.1
.

There holds

\displaystyle\left\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})\partial_{x}u_{\neq}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}),

(3.1)

\displaystyle\left\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})\partial_{y}u_{\neq}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(\|\partial_{y}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}),

\displaystyle\left\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})\partial_{z}u_{\neq}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(\|\partial_{z}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}),

\displaystyle\left\|(\partial_{x}^{2},\partial_{z}^{2})u_{3,\neq}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}),

\displaystyle\left\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})\partial_{x}\nabla u_{\neq}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(\|\partial_{x}\nabla\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}),

\displaystyle\left\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})\partial_{y}\nabla u_{\neq}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(\|\partial_{y}\nabla\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}).

Secondly, we present the nonlinear interactions between the non-zero modes of the velocity, which will often be used to estimate
E_{1}
,
E_{2}
and
E_{4}
.

Lemma 3.2
.

It holds that

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{2aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}|u_{\neq}|^{2}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq CA^{\frac{2}{3}}E_{2}^{4},

(3.2)

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{2aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq CA^{\frac{2}{3}}E_{2}^{4},

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{2aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_{x}(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{\neq})\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq CA^{\frac{1}{6}+\alpha}E_{2}^{4},

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{2aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\nabla(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{2,\neq})\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq CA^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2}{3}\alpha}E_{2}^{4},

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{2aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_{z}(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{3,\neq})\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq CA^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2}{3}\alpha}E_{2}^{4},

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{2aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_{z}(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{1,\neq})\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq CA^{\frac{4}{3}}E_{2}^{4},

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{2aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\nabla(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{3,\neq})\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(A^{\frac{7}{6}+\frac{1}{3}\alpha}E_{2}^{2}E_{5}^{2}+A^{\frac{4}{3}}E_{2}^{4}),

where
\alpha
is a constant with
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{4}).

Proof.

For convenience, we denote by

\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}=\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\Gamma_{2}=\|\nabla\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\Gamma_{3}=\|\partial_{x}\nabla\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\Gamma_{4}=\|\partial_{x}\nabla u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}.

According to the definition of
E_{2}(t)
and assumptions (
2.5
), there holds

\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\Gamma_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\frac{\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\Gamma_{1}\|_{L^{2}}}{A^{\frac{1}{6}}}+\frac{\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}(\Gamma_{2},\Gamma_{3})\|_{L^{2}}}{A^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\Gamma_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}}{A^{\frac{1}{3}}}+\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\Gamma_{4}\|_{L^{2}}\leq CE_{2},

which will be frequently used in later calculations.

Estimate of
(\ref{eq:non-neq0})_{1}.

Using
\eqref{sob_result_2}_{5}
in Lemma
A.3
and
\eqref{eq:velocity embed}_{1}
in Lemma
3.1
, we have

\displaystyle\|u_{\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}L^{2}_{y}}^{2}\leq C\big(\|\partial_{x}u_{\neq}\|^{2\alpha}_{L^{2}}\|u_{\neq}\|^{2-2\alpha}_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{x}\partial_{z}u_{\neq}\|^{2\alpha}_{L^{2}}\|u_{\neq}\|^{2-2\alpha}_{L^{2}}\big)\leq C\Gamma_{1}^{2}.

Therefore, by
\eqref{sob_result_2}_{8}
and
\eqref{eq:velocity embed}_{2},
we arrive

\displaystyle\||u_{\neq}|^{2}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\big(\|\partial_{x}u_{\neq}\|^{2\alpha}_{L^{2}}\|u_{\neq}\|^{2-2\alpha}_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{x}\partial_{z}u_{\neq}\|^{2\alpha}_{L^{2}}\|u_{\neq}\|^{2-2\alpha}_{L^{2}}\big)\|\nabla u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}\|u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\Gamma_{1}^{3}\Gamma_{2},

which indicates that

\|{\rm e}^{2aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}|u_{\neq}|^{2}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\leq CA^{\frac{2}{3}}(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{4}_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{4}_{X_{a}})\leq CA^{\frac{2}{3}}E_{2}^{4}.

Estimate of
(\ref{eq:non-neq0})_{2}.

Using Lemma
A.3
and Lemma
3.1
again, there hold

\displaystyle\|u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,y}L^{2}_{z}}^{2}\leq C\|\partial_{x}u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}\nabla u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\Gamma_{4}^{2},

\displaystyle\|u_{\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}L^{2}_{y}}^{2}\leq C\big(\|\partial_{x}u_{\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})\partial_{x}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)\leq C\Gamma_{1}^{2},

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y,z}}^{2}+\|\partial_{y}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{x,y}}^{2}\leq C\Gamma_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})u_{\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}L^{2}_{x,z}}^{2}\leq C\Gamma_{1}\Gamma_{2}.

Therefore, one gets

\displaystyle\|u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle\|u_{1,\neq}\partial_{x}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|u_{2,\neq}\partial_{y}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|u_{3,\neq}\partial_{z}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|u_{\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}L^{2}_{y}}^{2}\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})u_{\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}L^{2}_{x,z}}^{2}+\|u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,y}L^{2}_{z}}^{2}\|\partial_{y}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{x,y}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\Gamma_{1}^{3}\Gamma_{2}+\Gamma_{2}^{2}\Gamma_{4}^{2}),

which implies that

\|{\rm e}^{2aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}\leq CA^{\frac{2}{3}}E_{2}^{4}.

Estimate of
(\ref{eq:non-neq0})_{3}.

For
j\in\{1,3\},
by Lemma
A.3
and Lemma
3.1
, we have

\displaystyle\|u_{j,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}u_{j,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{x,y}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial_{x}(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})u_{j,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq C\Gamma_{1}^{2}

and

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}\partial_{j}u_{\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{y}L^{2}_{x,z}}+\|\partial_{j}u_{\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{x,y}L^{2}_{z}}\leq C\|\partial_{x}\partial_{j}\nabla u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}\partial_{j}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\Gamma_{1}\Gamma_{3},

which indicate that

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}(u_{j,\neq}\partial_{j}u_{\neq})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\Gamma_{1}^{3}\Gamma_{3}.

(3.3)

Using
\eqref{sob_result_2}_{1}
,
\eqref{sob_result_2}_{4}
and
\eqref{sob_result_2}_{7}
, there hold

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{x,y}L^{2}_{z}}+\|u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\|\partial_{x}\nabla u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{3-2\alpha}\|\nabla\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2\alpha-1}\leq C\Gamma_{4}^{3-2\alpha}\Gamma_{3}^{2\alpha-1}

and

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y}u_{\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{x,y}}\leq C\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})\partial_{y}u_{\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq C\Gamma_{2}^{2},

which show that

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}(u_{2,\neq}\partial_{y}u_{\neq})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\Gamma_{2}^{2}\Gamma_{3}^{2\alpha-1}\Gamma_{4}^{3-2\alpha}.

(3.4)

Combining (
3.3
) with (
3.4
), we conclude that

\|{\rm e}^{2aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_{x}(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{\neq})\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}\leq CA^{\frac{1}{6}+\alpha}E_{2}^{4}.

Estimate of
(\ref{eq:non-neq0})_{4}.

By Lemma
A.3
and Lemma
3.1
, direct calculations show that

\displaystyle\|u_{\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\leq C(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}})(\|\nabla\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}})\leq C\Gamma_{1}\Gamma_{2},

(3.5)

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{x,y}}+\|\nabla u_{\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y,z}}\leq C\Gamma_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{x,y}L^{2}_{z}}\leq C\|\partial_{x}\nabla u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{2\alpha-1}_{L^{2}}\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{2-2\alpha}_{L^{2}}\leq C\Gamma_{4}\Gamma_{3}^{2\alpha-1}\Gamma_{1}^{2-2\alpha}.

Combining above results with

\displaystyle\|\nabla(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{2,\neq})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\nabla u_{\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{x,y}}\|\nabla u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{x,y}L^{2}_{z}}+\|u_{\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle\leq C(\Gamma_{4}\Gamma_{3}^{2\alpha-1}\Gamma_{1}^{2-2\alpha}\Gamma_{2}^{2}+\Gamma_{1}^{3}\Gamma_{2}),

there holds

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{2aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\nabla(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{2,\neq})\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}\leq A^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2}{3}\alpha}E_{2}^{4}.

Estimate of
(\ref{eq:non-neq0})_{5}.

For
j\in\{1,3\}
, using Lemma
A.3
and Lemma
3.1
, we have

\displaystyle\|\partial_{z}u_{j,\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y,z}}^{2}\leq C\Gamma_{1}^{2},\quad\|\partial_{j}u_{3,\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y,z}L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\leq C\Gamma_{1}\Gamma_{3},

which along with
\eqref{ap:u11}_{1}
indicates that

\displaystyle\|\partial_{z}(u_{j,\neq}\partial_{j}u_{3,\neq})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\Gamma_{1}^{3}\Gamma_{3}.

(3.6)

For
j=2,
by Lemma
3.1
and Lemma
A.3
, there holds

\displaystyle\|u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\|\partial_{x}\nabla u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{3-2\alpha}\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2\alpha-1}\leq C\Gamma_{4}^{3-2\alpha}\Gamma_{1}^{2\alpha-1},

(3.7)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y}u_{3,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{x,y}}\leq C\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})\nabla u_{3,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq C\Gamma_{2}^{2},

which along with
\eqref{eq:velocity embed}_{2}
and
\eqref{ap:u11}_{3}
shows that

\displaystyle\|\partial_{z}(u_{2,\neq}\partial_{y}u_{3,\neq})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C(\Gamma_{4}\Gamma_{3}^{2\alpha-1}\Gamma_{1}^{2-2\alpha}\Gamma_{2}^{2}+\Gamma_{4}^{3-2\alpha}\Gamma_{1}^{2\alpha-1}\Gamma_{2}^{2}).

(3.8)

Combining (
3.6
) and (
3.8
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{2aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_{z}(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{3,\neq})\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\leq A^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2}{3}\alpha}E_{2}^{4}.

Estimate of
(\ref{eq:non-neq0})_{6}.

According to (
3.7
) and (
3.8
), after replacing
u_{3,\neq}
with
u_{1,\neq}
, we can prove that

\displaystyle\|\partial_{z}(u_{2,\neq}\partial_{y}u_{1,\neq})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C(\Gamma_{4}\Gamma_{3}^{2\alpha-1}\Gamma_{1}^{2-2\alpha}\Gamma_{2}^{2}+\Gamma_{4}^{3-2\alpha}\Gamma_{1}^{2\alpha-1}\Gamma_{2}^{2}).

(3.9)

Similar to (
3.6
), for
j\in\{1,3\},
by using
(\ref{eq:velocity embed})_{3},
one deduces

\displaystyle\|\partial_{z}(u_{j,\neq}\partial_{j}u_{1,\neq})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\Gamma_{1}\Gamma_{2}^{2}\Gamma_{3}.

(3.10)

Collecting (
3.9
) and (
3.10
), there holds

\|{\rm e}^{2aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_{z}(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{1,\neq})\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}\leq CA^{\frac{4}{3}}E_{2}^{4}.

Estimate of
(\ref{eq:non-neq0})_{7}.

For
j\in\{1,3\}
, using Lemma
A.3
and Lemma
3.1
again, we have

\displaystyle\|\partial_{j}u_{3,\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y,z}L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\leq C\|\partial_{z}\partial_{j}\nabla u_{3,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{z}\partial_{j}u_{3,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\Gamma_{1}\Gamma_{3},

which along with
\eqref{ap:u11}_{1}
and
\eqref{ap:u11}_{2}
indicates that

\displaystyle\|\nabla(u_{j,\neq}\partial_{j}u_{3,\neq})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|u_{j,\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|\nabla\partial_{j}u_{3,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla u_{j,\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y,z}}^{2}\|\partial_{j}u_{3,\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y,z}L^{2}_{x}}^{2})

(3.11)

\displaystyle\leq C(\Gamma_{1}\Gamma_{2}^{3}+\Gamma_{2}^{2}\Gamma_{1}\Gamma_{3}).

According to
\eqref{ap:u11}_{3}
and (
3.7
), one obtains

\displaystyle\|\nabla(u_{2,\neq}\partial_{y}u_{3,\neq})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|\partial_{y}\nabla u_{3,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,y}L^{2}_{z}}^{2}\|\partial_{y}u_{3,\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{x,y}}^{2})

(3.12)

\displaystyle\leq C(\Gamma_{4}^{3-2\alpha}\Gamma_{1}^{2\alpha-1}\|\triangle u_{3,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\Gamma_{4}\Gamma_{3}^{2\alpha-1}\Gamma_{1}^{2-2\alpha}\Gamma_{2}^{2}).

Therefore, we infer from (
3.11
) and (
3.12
) that

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{2aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\nabla(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{3,\neq})\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}\leq C(A^{\frac{7}{6}+\frac{1}{3}\alpha}E_{2}^{2}E_{5}^{2}+A^{\frac{4}{3}}E_{2}^{4}).

We finish the proof.
∎

Next, we give the nonlinear interaction between the zero mode
u_{1,0}
and the non-zero mode
u_{\neq}
.
Recall that the zero mode
u_{1,0}
is decomposed into two components with
u_{1,0}=\mathbf{U}_{1}+\mathbf{U}_{2},

where
\mathbf{U}_{1}
is the good component and
\mathbf{U}_{2}
is the bad component.

The following result will be used in estimating the energies
\{\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}^{2},\|\nabla\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}^{2}\}
.
Consequently, to close the energy estimates, the degree of
A
must be strictly less than 1.

Lemma 3.3
.

It holds that

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\mathbf{U}_{1}\partial_{x}\nabla u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\leq CA^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}^{2},

(3.13)

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\mathbf{U}_{1}\partial_{x}(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})u_{3,\neq}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\leq CA^{\frac{2}{3}}\|\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}),

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\mathbf{U}_{1}\partial_{x}^{2}u_{1,\neq}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\leq CA^{\frac{2}{3}}\|\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}),

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\mathbf{U}_{1}\partial_{x}\partial_{z}u_{1,\neq}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\leq CA^{\frac{2}{3}}\|\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}\left(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}\right),

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}(\partial_{y},\partial_{z})\mathbf{U}_{1}\partial_{x}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\leq CA^{\frac{2}{3}}\|\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}),

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_{y}\mathbf{U}_{1}\partial_{y}u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\leq CA^{\frac{2}{3}}\|\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}.

Proof.

Using Lemma
A.2
and Lemma
A.3
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{U}_{1}\partial_{x}\nabla u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{y}}^{2}\|\partial_{x}\nabla u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}L^{2}_{x,z}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}(\|\partial_{x}\nabla u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}\partial_{y}\nabla u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{x}\nabla u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}),

which implies that

\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\mathbf{U}_{1}\partial_{x}\nabla u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\leq CA^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}^{2}.

For
j\in\{1,3\},
by Lemma
A.2
, Lemma
A.3
and Lemma
3.1
, there holds

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{U}_{1}\partial_{x}(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})u_{j,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\|\partial_{x}(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})u_{j,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})\nabla u_{j,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}})(\|\partial_{x}\nabla\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}})

and

\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\mathbf{U}_{1}\partial_{x}(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})u_{j,\neq}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\leq CA^{\frac{2}{3}}\|\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}),

which give
\eqref{lemma_neq2_2}_{2}
,
\eqref{lemma_neq2_2}_{3}
and
\eqref{lemma_neq2_2}_{4}
.

Using Lemma
A.3
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\|(\partial_{y},\partial_{z})\mathbf{U}_{1}\partial_{x}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\partial_{x}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y,z}L^{2}_{x}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\partial_{x}(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})\nabla u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}})(\|\partial_{x}\nabla\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}),

which indicates that

\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1}\partial_{x}u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\leq CA^{\frac{2}{3}}\|\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}).

Using Lemma
A.3
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y}\mathbf{U}_{1}\partial_{y}u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\partial_{y}u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y,z}L^{2}_{x}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}

and

\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_{y}\mathbf{U}_{1}\partial_{y}u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\leq CA^{\frac{2}{3}}\|\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}.

∎

The following result is only used to estimate
\|(\partial_{y},\partial_{z})\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}^{2}
.
Hence, as long as the degree of
A
is less than
\frac{5}{3}
, the energy estimates become achievable.
Sometimes, we need to use the results of Lemma
4.1
, and we will prove them in next section.

Lemma 3.4
.

Under the conditions of Theorem
1.1
and the assumptions (
2.5
), there exists a constant
A_{3}
independent of
A
and
t
, such that if
A>A_{3},

it holds that

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}(\partial_{y},\partial_{z})(u_{2,\neq}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1})\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}\leq C\big(A^{\frac{2}{3}}\|\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}E_{2}^{2}+AE_{2}^{2\alpha}E_{4}^{2-2\alpha}\big),

(3.14)

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_{z}(u_{3,\neq}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1})\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}\leq C\big(A^{\frac{2}{3}}\|\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}E_{2}^{2}+A^{\frac{4}{3}}E_{2}^{2\alpha}E_{4}^{2-2\alpha}\big),

where
\alpha
is a constant with
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{4}).

Proof.

First, for
j\in\{2,3\},
direct calculations show that

\displaystyle\|\partial_{j}(u_{2,\neq}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq C\left(\|\partial_{j}u_{2,\neq}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|u_{2,\neq}\partial_{j}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right),

(3.15)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{z}(u_{3,\neq}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq C\left(\|\partial_{z}u_{3,\neq}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|u_{3,\neq}\partial_{z}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right).

By Lemma
A.3
, there hold

\displaystyle\|\partial_{j}u_{2,\neq}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq C\|\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}

and

\displaystyle\|\partial_{z}u_{3,\neq}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})u_{3,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})\partial_{y}u_{3,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}})(\|\partial_{x}\nabla\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}),

which indicate that

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_{j}u_{2,\neq}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}\leq CA^{\frac{2}{3}}\|\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}^{2}\leq CA^{\frac{2}{3}}\|\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}E_{2}^{2},

(3.16)

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_{z}u_{3,\neq}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}\leq CA^{\frac{2}{3}}\|\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}^{2}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}^{2})\leq CA^{\frac{2}{3}}\|\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}E_{2}^{2}.

Then, we need to deal with
\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}u_{2,\neq}\partial_{j}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}
and

\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}u_{3,\neq}\partial_{z}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}.

Since it is difficult to estimate
\|\partial_{j}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}
,
the traditional Sobolev embedding is insufficient to handle
\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}u_{2,\neq}\partial_{j}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}
and

\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}u_{3,\neq}\partial_{z}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}.

According to (
2.2
) and (
2.3
),
\mathbf{U}_{1}
satisfies

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}_{1}-\frac{1}{A}\triangle\mathbf{U}_{1}=\frac{1}{A}\widetilde{n}_{0}-\frac{1}{A}(u_{2,0}\partial_{y}\mathbf{U}_{1}+u_{3,0}\partial_{z}\mathbf{U}_{1})-\frac{1}{A}(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{1,\neq})_{0},\\
\mathbf{U}_{1}|_{t=0}=(u_{1,\rm in})_{0}.\end{array}\right.

(3.17)

Therefore, for the given positive constant
\epsilon_{1},
we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}({\rm e}^{-\epsilon_{1}A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\mathbf{U}_{1})+\frac{\epsilon_{1}{\rm e}^{-\epsilon_{1}A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\mathbf{U}_{1}}{A^{\frac{1}{3}}}-\frac{{\rm e}^{-\epsilon_{1}A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\triangle\mathbf{U}_{1}}{A}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm e}^{-\epsilon_{1}A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\widetilde{n}_{0}}{A}-\frac{{\rm e}^{-\epsilon_{1}A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}u_{j,0}\partial_{j}\mathbf{U}_{1}}{A}-\frac{{\rm e}^{-\epsilon_{1}A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{1,\neq})_{0}}{A},

where
j\in\{2,3\}.

Multiplying
-\frac{{\rm e}^{-\epsilon_{1}A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\triangle\mathbf{U}_{1}}{2}
on both sides of the above equation, the energy estimate shows that

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{-\epsilon_{1}A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\|{\rm e}^{-\epsilon_{1}A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\triangle\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}}{A}

(3.18)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|u_{1,\rm in}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\frac{C\big(\|{\rm e}^{-\epsilon_{1}A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\widetilde{n}_{0}\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}+\|{\rm e}^{-\epsilon_{1}A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}u_{j,0}\partial_{j}\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}+\|{\rm e}^{-\epsilon_{1}A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{1,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}\big)}{A}.

When
A>A_{2},
by Lemma
A.2
and Lemma
4.1
, we get

\displaystyle\|u_{2,0}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}}^{2}+\|u_{3,0}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}}^{2}\leq C(\|u_{2,0}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{2}}^{2}+\|u_{3,0}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2})\leq C\epsilon^{2},

(3.19)

which implies that

\frac{\|{\rm e}^{-\epsilon_{1}A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}u_{j,0}\partial_{j}\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}}{A}\leq\frac{C\epsilon^{2}\|{\rm e}^{-\epsilon_{1}A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\triangle\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}}{A}.

(3.20)

Using
\eqref{eq:non-neq0}_{2}
and (
3.20
), we infer from (
3.18
) that

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{-\epsilon_{1}A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\|{\rm e}^{-\epsilon_{1}A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\triangle\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}}{A}\leq C\Big(\|u_{1,\rm in}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\frac{\|{\rm e}^{-\epsilon_{1}A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\widetilde{n}_{0}\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}}{A}+\frac{E_{2}^{4}}{A^{\frac{1}{3}}}\Big).

Due to

\|\widetilde{n}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\|n_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\|n\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}}\|n_{0}\|_{L^{1}}\leq CE_{3}M

and

\|{\rm e}^{-\epsilon_{1}A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T)}\leq\frac{A^{\frac{1}{3}}}{2\epsilon_{1}},

there holds

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{-\epsilon_{1}A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\|{\rm e}^{-\epsilon_{1}A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\triangle\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}}{A}\leq C\Big(\|u_{1,\rm in}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\frac{E_{3}M}{\epsilon_{1}A^{\frac{2}{3}}}+\frac{E_{2}^{4}}{A^{\frac{1}{3}}}\Big).

(3.21)

When
A\geq\max\{A_{2},{\epsilon_{1}}^{-\frac{3}{2}}M^{\frac{3}{2}}E_{3}^{\frac{3}{2}},E_{2}^{12}\}=:A_{3},
by
\eqref{sob_result_2}_{3}
,
(
3.21
)
and Lemma
3.1
, we have

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}u_{2,\neq}\partial_{j}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{2\alpha}_{X_{a}}\|u_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{b}}^{2-2\alpha}\|{\rm e}^{-2(1-\alpha)(b-a)A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\triangle\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}

(3.22)

\displaystyle\leq CAE_{2}^{2\alpha}E_{4}^{2-2\alpha},

where
\alpha
is a constant satisfying
\alpha\in\big(\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{4}\big).

By
\eqref{sob_result_2}_{3}
and Lemma
3.1
, there holds

\displaystyle\|u_{3,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{y,z}L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})u_{3,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})u_{3,\neq}\|^{2\alpha-1}_{L^{2}}\|u_{3,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2-2\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\partial_{y}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}})(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}})^{2\alpha-1}\|u_{3,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2-2\alpha},

which along with
(
3.21
)
implies that

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}u_{3,\neq}\partial_{z}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\partial_{y}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}})(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}})^{2\alpha-1}

(3.23)

\displaystyle\cdot\|\partial_{x}^{2}u_{3,\neq}\|_{X_{b}}^{2-2\alpha}\|{\rm e}^{-2(1-\alpha)(b-a)A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\triangle\mathbf{U}_{1}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq CA^{\frac{4}{3}}E_{2}^{2\alpha}E_{4}^{2-2\alpha}.

Collecting (
3.15
), (
3.16
), (
3.22
) and (
3.23
), we finish the proof.

∎

Furthermore, we show the the nonlinear interactions between the bad component
\mathbf{U}_{2}
of
u_{1,0}
and the non-zero mode
u_{\neq}
.

Lemma 3.5
.

For
j\in\{2,3\}
, there holds

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{2}\partial_{x}u_{j,\neq}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\leq CAE_{1}^{2}E_{4}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}})^{\frac{3}{2}},

(3.24)

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_{z}\mathbf{U}_{2}\partial_{x}^{2}u_{1,\neq}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\leq CA^{\frac{17}{12}}E_{1}^{2}E_{4}^{\frac{1}{4}}(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}})^{\frac{7}{4}},

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_{j}\mathbf{U}_{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{j}u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\leq CA^{\frac{4}{3}}E_{1}^{2}E_{4}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}^{\frac{3}{2}},

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_{z}(u_{2,\neq}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{2})\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}\leq CAE_{1}^{2}E_{4}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}^{\frac{3}{2}},

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_{z}(u_{3,\neq}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{2})\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}\leq CE_{1}^{2}A^{\frac{4}{3}}E_{4}(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}).

Proof.

First of all, there holds

\displaystyle\|\partial_{j}\mathbf{U}_{2}\|_{H^{1}}\leq\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{s}\partial_{j}\mathbf{U}_{2}(s)\|_{H^{1}.}ds\leq CE_{1}t.

For the given positive constant
\epsilon_{2},
since

\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t{\rm e}^{-\epsilon_{2}A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}=0,

there holds

\big\|{\rm e}^{-\epsilon_{2}A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\|\partial_{j}\mathbf{U}_{2}\|_{H^{1}}\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}}\leq CA^{\frac{1}{3}}E_{1}.

(3.25)

By Lemma
A.2
and Lemma
A.3
, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla\mathbf{U}_{2}\partial_{x}u_{j,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\|\nabla\mathbf{U}_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}L^{2}_{z}}^{2}\|\partial_{x}u_{j,\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{x,y}}^{2}\leq C\|\nabla\mathbf{U}_{2}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\|\partial_{x}u_{j,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{x}\partial_{z}u_{j,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{2}},

which along with (
3.25
) and Lemma
3.1
indicates that

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{2}\partial_{x}u_{j,\neq}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}{\rm e}^{\frac{a-b}{2}A^{-\frac{1}{3}}s}\|\nabla\mathbf{U}_{2}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}{\rm e}^{\frac{b+3a}{2}A^{-\frac{1}{3}}s}\|\partial_{x}^{2}u_{j,\neq}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}\partial_{z}u_{j,\neq}\|^{\frac{3}{2}}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq CAE_{1}^{2}E_{4}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}})^{\frac{3}{2}}.

Similarly, one obtains that

\displaystyle\|\partial_{z}\mathbf{U}_{2}\partial_{x}^{2}u_{1,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\|\partial_{z}\mathbf{U}_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{y}}^{2}\|\partial_{x}^{2}u_{1,\neq}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}L^{2}_{x,z}}^{2}\leq C\|\partial_{z}\mathbf{U}_{2}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\|\partial_{x}^{2}u_{1,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}^{2}\nabla u_{1,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial_{z}\mathbf{U}_{2}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\|\partial_{x}^{2}u_{1,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{4}}(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}})^{\frac{3}{4}}(\|\partial_{x}\nabla\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}).

By the divergence-free property

\partial_{x}^{2}u_{1,\neq}=-\partial_{x}(\partial_{y}u_{2,\neq}+\partial_{z}u_{3,\neq}),

we can prove the second result.

Using Lemma
A.2
and Lemma
A.3
again, by Hölder’s inequality, there is

\displaystyle\|\partial_{j}\mathbf{U}_{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{j}u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial_{j}\mathbf{U}_{2}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\|\partial_{x}\partial_{j}u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}\partial_{j}\nabla u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial_{j}\mathbf{U}_{2}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\|\partial_{x}^{2}u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{x}\partial_{j}\nabla u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}},

which implies that

\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_{j}\mathbf{U}_{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{j}u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\leq CA^{\frac{4}{3}}E_{1}^{2}E_{4}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}^{\frac{3}{2}}.

According to Lemma
A.2
and Lemma
A.3
, by Hölder’s inequality, we get

\displaystyle\|\partial_{z}(u_{2,\neq}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{2})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla\mathbf{U}_{2}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\|\nabla u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla\mathbf{U}_{2}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\|u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{2}},

which indicates that

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_{z}(u_{2,\neq}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{2})\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big\|{\rm e}^{\frac{a-b}{4}A^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\|\nabla\mathbf{U}_{2}\|_{H^{1}}\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}}^{2}\|{\rm e}^{bA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{2}}\leq CE_{1}^{2}E_{4}^{\frac{1}{2}}A\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}^{\frac{3}{2}}.

Using Lemma
A.3
, there holds

\displaystyle\|\partial_{z}u_{3,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{y}L^{2}_{x,z}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial_{z}\partial_{y}u_{3,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{z}u_{3,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|u_{3,\neq}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{z}^{2}u_{3,\neq}\|^{{}^{\frac{1}{2}}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{y}u_{3,\neq}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{y}\partial_{z}^{2}u_{3,\neq}\|^{{}^{\frac{1}{2}}}_{L^{2}}

and

\displaystyle\|u_{3,\neq}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{y,z}L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})\partial_{y}u_{3,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}\|(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})u_{3,\neq}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|u_{3,\neq}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|(\partial_{x}^{2},\partial_{z}^{2})u_{3,\neq}\|^{{}^{\frac{1}{2}}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{y}u_{3,\neq}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{y}(\partial_{x}^{2},\partial_{z}^{2})u_{3,\neq}\|^{{}^{\frac{1}{2}}}_{L^{2}}.

Using Lemma
3.1
, we get

\displaystyle\quad\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_{z}(u_{3,\neq}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{2})\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}\leq CE_{1}^{2}A^{\frac{4}{3}}E_{4}(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}).

∎

Lastly, we show the the nonlinear interaction between the zero modes
\{u_{2,0},u_{3,0}\}
and the non-zero mode
u_{\neq}
.
The proof can be found in Lemma 3.16 of
[
8
]
, and we omit it.

Lemma 3.6
.

For
j\in\{2,3\}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}u_{j,0}(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})\nabla u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\leq CA(\|u_{2,0}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}H^{2}}+\|u_{3,0}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}H^{1}})(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}),

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_{z}\nabla u_{j,0}\cdot u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\leq CA^{\frac{2}{3}}(\|u_{2,0}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}H^{2}}+\|u_{3,0}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}H^{1}})(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}),

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_{z}u_{j,0}\nabla u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\leq CA(\|u_{2,0}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}H^{2}}+\|u_{3,0}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}H^{1}})(\|\partial_{x}\omega_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}+\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|^{2}_{X_{a}}),

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_{y}u_{3,0}(\partial_{x},\partial_{z})u_{2,\neq}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\leq CA^{\frac{1}{3}}\left(\|u_{2,0}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{2}}^{2}+\|u_{3,0}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}\right)\|\triangle u_{2,\neq}\|_{X_{a}}^{2}.\quad\quad\quad\quad

4.
Estimates for the zero modes of velocity
E_{1}(t)
: Proof of Proposition
2.1

In this section, we give some estimates for the zero modes of the velocity, which will be used in estimating the zero mode of the density and the non-zero modes.

4.1.
Energy estimates for
u_{2,0}
and
u_{3,0}

We recall that

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\partial_{t}u_{2,0}-\frac{1}{A}\triangle u_{2,0}+\frac{1}{A}(u\cdot\nabla u_{2})_{0}+\frac{1}{A}\partial_{y}P^{N_{1}}_{0}+\frac{1}{A}\partial_{y}P^{N_{2}}_{0}=0,\\
\partial_{t}u_{3,0}-\frac{1}{A}\triangle u_{3,0}+\frac{1}{A}(u\cdot\nabla u_{3})_{0}+\frac{1}{A}\partial_{z}P^{N_{1}}_{0}+\frac{1}{A}\partial_{z}P^{N_{2}}_{0}=0,\\
\partial_{y}u_{2,0}+\partial_{z}u_{3,0}=0,\end{array}\right.

(4.1)

where

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\triangle P^{N_{1}}=-2A\partial_{x}u_{2}+\partial_{x}n,\\
\triangle P^{N_{2}}=-{\rm div}~(u\cdot\nabla u).\end{array}\right.

Lemma 4.1
.

Under the conditions of Theorem
1.1
and the assumptions (
1.6
) and (
2.5
), there exists a positive constant
A_{2,3}
independent of
A
and
t
, such that if
A\geq A_{2,3},

it holds that

\displaystyle\|u_{2,0}\|_{Y_{0}}+\|u_{3,0}\|_{Y_{0}}\leq C\epsilon,

(4.2)

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{2,0}\|_{Y_{0}}+\|\nabla u_{3,0}\|_{Y_{0}}\leq C\epsilon,

\displaystyle\|\triangle u_{2,0}\|_{Y_{0}}\leq C\epsilon,

\displaystyle\|\min\{(A^{-\frac{2}{3}}+A^{-1}t)^{\frac{1}{2}},1\}\triangle u_{3,0}\|_{Y_{0}}\leq C\epsilon.

Proof.

Estimate of
(\ref{eq:u20u30})_{1}.
Due to
\nabla\cdot u_{0}=0
, we have

<\partial_{y}P^{N_{k}}_{0},u_{2,0}>+<\partial_{z}P^{N_{k}}_{0},u_{3,0}>=-<P^{N_{k}}_{0},\partial_{y}u_{2,0}+\partial_{z}u_{3,0}>=0,~~{\rm for}~~k\in\{1,2\}

<u_{0}\cdot\nabla u_{2,0},u_{2,0}>+<u_{0}\cdot\nabla u_{3,0},u_{3,0}>=0.

When
A\geq\max\{1,\epsilon^{-6}E_{2}^{12}\}=:A_{2,1},

using Lemma
3.2
and assumption (
1.6
),
energy estimates of (
4.1
) show that

\displaystyle\|u_{2,0}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}+\|u_{3,0}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{A}\left(\|\nabla u_{2,0}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla u_{3,0}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|(u_{2,{\rm in}})_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|(u_{3,{\rm in}})_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{C\||u_{\neq}|^{2}\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}}{A}\leq\|(u_{2,{\rm in}})_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|(u_{3,{\rm in}})_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{CE_{2}^{4}}{A^{\frac{1}{3}}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}.

Estimate of
(\ref{eq:u20u30})_{2}.

Multiplying
2\triangle u_{2,0}
on
(\ref{u_zero_1})_{1}
and

2\triangle u_{3,0}
on
(\ref{u_zero_1})_{2},
energy estimates give that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\|\nabla u_{2,0}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla u_{3,0}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)+\frac{1}{A}\left(\|\triangle u_{2,0}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{3,0}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)

(4.3)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\frac{\|u_{0}\cdot\nabla u_{2,0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|u_{0}\cdot\nabla u_{3,0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{A},

where we use

<\partial_{y}P_{0}^{N_{k}},\triangle u_{2,0}>+<\partial_{z}P_{0}^{N_{k}},\triangle u_{3,0}>=0
for
k\in\{1,2\}
.

For
j\in\{2,3\},
by Hölder’s inequality and Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality, there holds

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{j,0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\|u_{j,0}\|_{L^{2}}\|\triangle u_{j,0}\|_{L^{2}},

(4.4)

\displaystyle\|u_{0}\cdot\nabla u_{j,0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\|(u_{2},u_{3})_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla(u_{2},u_{3})_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla u_{j,0}\|_{L^{2}}\|\triangle u_{j,0}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+C\|(u_{2},u_{3})_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\nabla u_{j,0}\|_{L^{2}}\|\triangle u_{j,0}\|_{L^{2}}.

According to (
4.4
) and Young’s inequality, we rewrite (
4.3
) into

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\nabla(u_{2},u_{3})_{0}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2A}\left(\frac{\|\nabla u_{2,0}\|_{L^{2}}^{4}}{\|u_{2,0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}+\frac{\|\nabla u_{3,0}\|_{L^{2}}^{4}}{\|u_{3,0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}\right)+\frac{C\|u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{A}

(4.5)

\displaystyle+C\frac{\|(u_{2},u_{3})_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\nabla(u_{2},u_{3})_{0}\|^{4}_{L^{2}}+\|(u_{2},u_{3})_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\|\nabla(u_{2},u_{3})_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{A}.

Thanks to
(\ref{eq:u20u30})_{1}
, by taking
\epsilon
small enough, we infer from (
4.5
) that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\nabla(u_{2},u_{3})_{0}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq C\frac{\|u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{A}+C\frac{\|(u_{2},u_{3})_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\|\nabla(u_{2},u_{3})_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{A}.

(4.6)

From this, along with Lemma
3.2
, (
1.6
) and
(\ref{eq:u20u30})_{1}
, when
A\geq A_{2,1},
one deduces

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{2,0}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+\|\nabla u_{3,0}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|(u_{2,{\rm in}})_{0}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|(u_{3,{\rm in}})_{0}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\frac{C\|u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}}{A}+C\epsilon^{6}

(4.7)

\displaystyle\leq\|(u_{2,{\rm in}})_{0}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|(u_{3,{\rm in}})_{0}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\frac{CE_{2}^{4}}{A^{\frac{1}{3}}}+C\epsilon^{6}\leq C\epsilon^{2}.

By Lemma
A.2
,
(\ref{eq:u20u30})_{1}
and (
4.7
), we have

\|u_{0}\cdot\nabla u_{j,0}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\|(u_{2},u_{3})_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}\|\triangle u_{j,0}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\epsilon^{2}\|\triangle u_{j,0}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}.

Integrating in time for (
4.3
) and taking
\epsilon
small enough, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{2,0}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+\|\nabla u_{3,0}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+\frac{\|\triangle u_{2,0}\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}+\|\triangle u_{3,0}\|^{2}_{L^{2}L^{2}}}{A}

(4.8)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\|(u_{2,{\rm in}})_{0}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|(u_{3,{\rm in}})_{0}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\|u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{\neq}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}}{A}\right)\leq C\epsilon^{2}.

Estimate of
(\ref{eq:u20u30})_{3}.

The
H^{2}
energy estimate for
(\ref{u_zero_1})_{1}
shows that

\displaystyle\|\triangle u_{2,0}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\|\nabla\triangle u_{2,0}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}}{A}

(4.9)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|(u_{2,\rm in})_{0}\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\frac{C}{A}\left(\|\nabla(u_{0}\cdot\nabla u_{2,0})\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{2,\neq})\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}+\|\triangle P_{0}^{N_{2}}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\right).

Using Lemma
A.2
,
(\ref{eq:u20u30})_{1,2}
and
\partial_{z}u_{3,0}=-\partial_{y}u_{2,0},
for
j\in\{2,3\},
there holds

\displaystyle\frac{\|\nabla(u_{0}\cdot\nabla u_{j,0})\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}}{A}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{A}\left(\|u_{2,0}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{2}}^{2}+\|u_{3,0}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}\right)\left(\|\nabla u_{2,0}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\nabla u_{3,0}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}\right)

(4.10)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\epsilon^{4}+C\epsilon^{2}\|\triangle u_{2,0}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}.

Moreover, due to
{\rm div}~(u\cdot\nabla u)=\partial_{x}(u\cdot\nabla u_{1})+\partial_{y}(u\cdot\nabla u_{2})+\partial_{z}(u\cdot\nabla u_{3}),
there holds

\displaystyle\|{\rm div}~(u\cdot\nabla u)_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\|\partial_{y}(u\cdot\nabla u_{2})_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\partial_{z}(u\cdot\nabla u_{3})_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y}(u_{0}\cdot\nabla u_{2,0})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\partial_{z}(u_{0}\cdot\nabla u_{3,0})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\partial_{y}(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{2,\neq})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\partial_{z}(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{3,\neq})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

From this, along with Lemma
3.2
and (
4.10
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\|\triangle P_{0}^{N_{2}}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}}{A}=\frac{\|{\rm div}~(u\cdot\nabla u)_{0}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}}{A}\leq C\left(\epsilon^{4}+\frac{E_{2}^{4}}{A^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2}{3}\alpha}}\right)+C\epsilon^{2}\|\triangle u_{2,0}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2},

(4.11)

where
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{4})
is a constant.

Then using assumption (
1.6
), (
4.10
), (
4.11
) and Lemma
3.2
, we get from (
4.9
) that

\displaystyle\|\triangle u_{2,0}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\|\nabla\triangle u_{2,0}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}}{A}\leq\epsilon^{2}+C\left(\epsilon^{4}+\frac{E_{2}^{4}}{A^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2}{3}\alpha}}\right)+C\epsilon^{2}\|\triangle u_{2,0}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}.

By taking
\epsilon
small enough, one obtains

\displaystyle\|\triangle u_{2,0}\|_{Y_{0}}^{2}\leq C\epsilon^{2}

provided with
A\geq\max\{A_{2,1},\big(E_{2}^{2}\epsilon^{-1}\big)^{\frac{12}{3-4\alpha}}\}=:A_{2.2}.

Estimate of
(\ref{eq:u20u30})_{4}.

Taking
H^{2}
energy estimate for
(\ref{u_zero_1})_{2}
, there holds

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\triangle u_{3,0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\|\nabla\triangle u_{3,0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{A}\leq\frac{C}{A}\left(\|\nabla(u_{0}\cdot\nabla u_{3,0})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{3,\neq})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\triangle P_{0}^{N_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right),

which follows that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\min\{A^{-\frac{2}{3}}+A^{-1}t,1\}\|\triangle u_{3,0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{\min\{A^{-\frac{2}{3}}+A^{-1}t,1\}}{A}\|\nabla\triangle u_{3,0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

(4.12)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{A}\left(\|\nabla(u_{0}\cdot\nabla u_{3,0})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+(A^{-\frac{2}{3}}+A^{-1}t)\|\nabla(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{3,\neq})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\triangle P_{0}^{N_{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\triangle u_{3,0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right).

Thanks to
(\ref{eq:u20u30})_{3}
, (
4.10
) and (
4.11
), we get

\displaystyle\frac{\|\nabla(u_{0}\cdot\nabla u_{j,0})\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}}{A}\leq C\epsilon^{4},\quad\frac{\|\triangle P_{0}^{N_{2}}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}}{A}\leq C\left(\epsilon^{4}+\frac{E_{2}^{4}}{A^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2}{3}\alpha}}\right).

(4.13)

Notice that
\|(A^{-\frac{2}{3}}+A^{-1}t){\rm e}^{-aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}}\leq CA^{-\frac{2}{3}},

using
(\ref{eq:u20u30})_{2},
(
4.13
) and Lemma
3.2
, one obtains

\displaystyle\|\min\{(A^{-\frac{2}{3}}+A^{-1}t)^{\frac{1}{2}},1\}\triangle u_{3,0}\|_{Y_{0}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\|(u_{3,\rm in})_{0}\|_{H^{2}}^{2}}{A^{\frac{2}{3}}}+C\left(\epsilon^{2}+\frac{E_{2}^{4}}{A^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2}{3}\alpha}}\right)+\frac{C}{A}\|(A^{-\frac{2}{3}}+A^{-1}t){\rm e}^{-aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}}\|{\rm e}^{2aA^{-\frac{1}{3}}t}\nabla(u_{\neq}\cdot\nabla u_{3,\neq})\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\|(u_{3,\rm in})_{0}\|_{H^{2}}^{2}}{A^{\frac{2}{3}}}+C\left(\epsilon^{2}+\frac{E_{2}^{4}}{A^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2}{3}\alpha}}+\frac{E_{2}^{2}E_{5}^{2}}{A^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{3}\alpha}}\right),\quad\alpha\in\Big(\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{4}\Big).

When

A\geq\max\{A_{2,2},(\|(u_{3,\rm in})_{0}\|_{H^{2}}\epsilon^{-1})^{3},(E_{2}E_{5}\epsilon^{-1})^{\frac{12}{3-2\alpha}}\}=:A_{2,3},

we infer from the above inequality that

\|\min\{(A^{-\frac{2}{3}}+A^{-1}t)^{\frac{1}{2}},1\}\triangle u_{3,0}\|_{Y_{0}}^{2}\leq C\epsilon^{2}.

The proof is complete.
∎

4.2.
Heat dissipation estimates for
u_{2,0}
and
u_{3,0}

Next, we consider the standard heat equation in
(y,z)\in\mathbb{T}^{2}:

\partial_{t}f-\frac{1}{A}\triangle f=g,\quad t\in[0,T],

(4.14)

where
g(t,y,z)
is a given function. By performing a decomposition similar to
(\ref{def:check{f_0}})
, we obtain the following heat dissipation estimate for the non-average part
\widetilde{f}=\sum_{k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}\neq 0}\widehat{f}_{k_{2},k_{3}}(t){\rm e}^{i(k_{2}y+k_{3}z)}
.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
f(t,y,z)
be a solution of (
4.14
), there holds

\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{A}\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\nabla\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left(\|f_{\rm in}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+A\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}(-\triangle)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{g}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\right),

where
\widetilde{g}=\sum_{k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}\neq 0}\widehat{g}_{k_{2},k_{3}}(t){\rm e}^{i(k_{2}y+k_{3}z)}.

Proof.

Doing the Fourier transform to (
4.14
), the Fourier mode
\widetilde{f}
satisfies

\partial_{t}\widehat{f}_{k_{2},k_{3}}+\frac{k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}}{A}\widehat{f}_{k_{2},k_{3}}=\widehat{g}_{k_{2},k_{3}},\quad k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}>0.

(4.15)

The solution of (
4.15
) is given by

\widehat{f}_{k_{2},k_{3}}={\rm e}^{-\frac{k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}}{A}t}(\widehat{f}_{{\rm in}})_{k_{2},k_{3}}+\int_{0}^{t}{\rm e}^{\frac{k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}}{A}(s-t)}\widehat{g}_{k_{2},k_{3}}(s)ds=:F_{(1)}+F_{(2)}.

(4.16)

For
F_{(1)},
due to
k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}>0,
there holds

|F_{(1)}|\leq{\rm e}^{-\frac{t}{2A}}|\widehat{f}_{\rm in}|,

which shows that

\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}F_{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T)}^{2}\leq|\widehat{f}_{\rm in}|^{2}.

For
F_{(2)},
using H
\ddot{\rm o}
lder’s inequality and
k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}>0,
we have

\displaystyle|F_{(2)}|\leq

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{\frac{k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}}{2A}(s-t)}\|_{L^{2}}\|{\rm e}^{\frac{k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}}{2A}(s-t)}\widehat{g}_{k_{2},k_{3}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{A}{k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\rm e}^{\frac{k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}}{2A}(s-t)}\widehat{g}_{k_{2},k_{3}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}\leq\left(\frac{A}{k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\rm e}^{\frac{s-t}{2A}}\widehat{g}_{k_{2},k_{3}}(s)\|_{L^{2}}.

This implies that

\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}F_{(2)}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T)}^{2}\leq CA(k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2})^{-1}\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\widehat{g}_{k_{2},k_{3}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T)}^{2}.

Collecting the estimates of
F_{(1)},F_{(2)}
, and using the Plancherel’s theorem, we get from (
4.16
) that

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}=

\displaystyle|\mathbb{T}|^{2}\sum_{k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}>0}\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\widehat{f}_{k_{2},k_{3}}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T)}^{2}

(4.17)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\mathbb{T}|^{2}\sum_{k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}>0}\left(\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}F_{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T)}^{2}+\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}F_{(2)}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T)}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C|\mathbb{T}|^{2}\sum_{k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}>0}\left(|(\widehat{f}_{\rm in})_{k_{2},k_{3}}|^{2}+A(k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2})^{-1}\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\widehat{g}_{k_{2},k_{3}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T)}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\|f_{\rm in}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+A\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}(-\triangle)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{g}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\right).

We also need to estimate
\frac{1}{A}\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\nabla\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}.
Multiplying (
4.15
) by
{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}},
the energy estimate gives that

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2A}\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\widehat{f}_{k_{2},k_{3}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{2(k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2})}{3A}\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\widehat{f}_{k_{2},k_{3}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq-\frac{1}{2}\left(|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\widehat{f}_{k_{2},k_{3}}|^{2}\right)|_{t=0}^{t=T}+\frac{CA\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\widehat{g}_{k_{2},k_{3}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}},

which follows that

\frac{k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}}{A}\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\widehat{f}_{k_{2},k_{3}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left(|(\widehat{f}_{\rm in})_{k_{2},k_{3}}|^{2}+A(k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2})^{-1}\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\widehat{g}_{k_{2},k_{3}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right).

From this, along with the Plancherel’s theorem, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{A}\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\nabla\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{A}|\mathbb{T}|^{2}\sum_{k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}>0}\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}(k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{f}_{k_{2},k_{3}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T)}^{2}

(4.18)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C|\mathbb{T}|^{2}\sum_{k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}>0}\left(|(\widehat{f}_{\rm in})_{k_{2},k_{3}}|^{2}+A(k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2})^{-1}\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\widehat{g}_{k_{2},k_{3}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\|f_{\rm in}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+A\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}(-\triangle)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{g}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\right).

Combining (
4.17
) with (
4.18
), one obtains

\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{A}\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\nabla\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left(\|f_{\rm in}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+A\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}(-\triangle)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{g}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\right).

The proof is complete.
∎

Similarly, we decompose the velocity
u_{2,0}
and
u_{3,0}
in the same way of (
2.1
), satisfying

\displaystyle u_{2,0}(t,y,z)=\overline{u}_{2,0}(t)+\widetilde{u}_{2,0}(t,y,z),

(4.19)

\displaystyle u_{3,0}(t,y,z)=\overline{u}_{3,0}(t)+\widetilde{u}_{3,0}(t,y,z).

In this way, combining with Lemma
4.2
, we can prove a typical heat dissipation estimates for
\widetilde{u}_{2,0}(t,y,z)
and
\widetilde{u}_{3,0}(t,y,z)
.
Furthermore, for
j\in\{2,3\},

\overline{u}_{j,0}(t)
is
a constant satisfying

\displaystyle\overline{u}_{j,0}(t)\equiv\frac{1}{|\mathbb{T}|^{2}}\int_{|\mathbb{T}|^{2}}(u_{j,\rm in})_{0}dydz.

Lemma 4.3
.

Under the assumptions of Lemma
4.1
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\widetilde{u}_{2,0}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\widetilde{u}_{3,0}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{A}\left(\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\nabla\widetilde{u}_{2,0}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\nabla\widetilde{u}_{3,0}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\right)\leq C\epsilon^{2}.

Proof.

Applying Lemma
4.2
to
(\ref{u_zero_1})_{1,2},
and noting that
\nabla\cdot u=0,
we get

\displaystyle\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\widetilde{u}_{2,0}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\widetilde{u}_{3,0}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{A}\left(\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\nabla\widetilde{u}_{2,0}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\nabla\widetilde{u}_{3,0}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\right)

(4.20)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\bigg(\|(u_{2,\rm in})_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|(u_{3,\rm in})_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{A}\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\widetilde{(u_{2}u_{2})}_{0}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{A}\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\large{\widetilde{(u_{3}u_{2})}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{A}\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\widetilde{(u_{3}u_{3})}_{0}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{A}\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\widetilde{P}^{N_{1}}_{0}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{A}\|{\rm e}^{\frac{t}{2A}}\widetilde{P}^{N_{2}}_{0}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle C\left(\|(u_{2,\rm in})_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|(u_{3,\rm in})_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+I_{1}+\cdots+I_{5}\right).

For
I_{1},
as
